Well, I'm here because... for months now, I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern in my project files at work. They're doing it to make it look like my quality control failed. And, I mean, I've brought it up, but even when IT looks into it and tells me the audit trail is completely clean, I know what I'm seeing. I know the digital record has been arranged specifically to put the blame on me.

No, I don't. I mean, like I just mentioned, I work in these project files every single day. I can see the exact pattern where the translation memory and the timestamps are being altered by this colleague and the subcontractors. It's too precise to be an accident or a glitch.

It's strictly related to my work. I don't have any issues like this with my family or friends, or... or anyone else in my personal life. Everything outside of the office is completely fine. It's really just that specific situation with the localization project and those particular people at my job.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about what's happening with the project files ever changed since this started?\n\nPatient: No, they really haven't. I know I'm kind of repeating myself, but for months I’ve just been seeing the exact same pattern in the project files. It's all set up so it looks like my quality control failed. And... well, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn't change what I know. I know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. So, no, my perspective on it has stayed exactly the same since this all began.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could potentially convince you that your colleague and subcontractors are not making these deliberate changes?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think so. I think... yes, if they knew how our software works, they absolutely would. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really. I mean... maybe for a brief second when IT first told me the audit trail was clean, I paused and wondered if I was missing something. But then I just go back and look at the files. 

Like I said earlier, I've been seeing this exact same pattern for months now. It's always those certain subcontractors and that one colleague making these tiny little changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. It's just so obvious that it's being done to make my quality control look like it failed. So, even when IT insists the system is clean, I know what I'm seeing. The record has been carefully arranged to put the blame on me. Because the proof is right there in front of me every time I log in, I don't really have any reason to doubt it.

I know the digital record has been arranged specifically to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever doubt that these changes are being made deliberately to blame you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I mean, like I just mentioned, I work in these project files every single day. I can see the exact pattern where the translation memory and the timestamps are being altered by this colleague and the subcontractors. It's too precise to be an accident or a glitch. \n\nSo when IT comes back and says the audit trail is clean, it doesn't make me doubt what I'm seeing—it just confirms to me that the digital record was carefully arranged to cover their tracks. The whole point is to make my quality control look like it's failing so they can put the blame on me. So... no, I really don't have any doubts about what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't believe your colleague and subcontractors were making these deliberate changes?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not since it started. Since that point, it's just been a constant issue, so there hasn't been a time where I didn't believe it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people in other areas of your life are out to get you or harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's strictly related to my work. I don't have any issues like this with my family or friends, or... or anyone else in my personal life. Everything outside of the office is completely fine. It's really just that specific situation with the localization project and those particular people at my job.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about what's happening with the project files ever changed since this started?\n\nPatient: No, they really haven't. I know I'm kind of repeating myself, but for months I’ve just been seeing the exact same pattern in the project files. It hasn't stopped or changed at all. \n\nCertain subcontractors and that one colleague just keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. It's all set up so it looks like my quality control failed. And... well, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn't change what I know. I know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. So, no, my perspective on it has stayed exactly the same since this all began.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could potentially convince you that your colleague and subcontractors are not making these deliberate changes?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think so. I mean, like I just mentioned, the proof is right there in front of me. I've been looking at this exact same pattern for months. I see the tiny changes they make to the translation memory and the timestamps with my own eyes. \n\nSo... if someone tries to tell me it's not happening, or if IT comes back again and says the audit trail is clean, it doesn't really matter. I already know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me and make my quality control look bad. When you can clearly see what they are doing to the files, there's really nothing that can convince you otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other thoughts or beliefs that you think might be hard for others to understand or agree with?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... no. Like I mentioned before, everything else in my life is completely normal. I don't have any unusual ideas or... beliefs about anything else. It's really just this one specific issue at work because I'm dealing with those actual files every day. Outside of that, no, nothing else comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a break or a period of time where you didn't think about these changes to the project files?\n\nPatient: No, there really hasn't been a break. It's been pretty constant. This has been going on for about 11 months now... basically since shortly after I was put in charge of the new localization project. So, no, I haven't really had a period where I wasn't thinking about it, because I have to look at those files every day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the deliberate changes to the project files could ever change?\n\nPatient: I really don't see how it could. I mean... I’m here talking to you because for months I’ve been seeing this exact same pattern in the project files. As long as those certain subcontractors and that one colleague keep making those tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps to make my quality control look like it failed, my mind isn't going to change. \n\nLike I mentioned before, even when IT tells me the audit trail is completely clean, I know the record has just been arranged to put the blame on me. So, no, unless they actually stop doing it and the files go back to normal, I don't see why I would think any differently about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that you might be mistaken about the changes to the project files?\n\nPatient: I... no. I really don't think I'm mistaken. I mean, like I just said, I'm here because for months I've been seeing this exact same pattern in the project files. It's not a mistake. Certain subcontractors and that one colleague are definitely making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. They're doing it specifically so it looks like my quality control failed. And I know IT says the audit trail is clean, but... well, I know the record has just been arranged to put the blame on me. It's too deliberate to be a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the changes to the project files could be considered an opinion or is it an absolute fact to you?\n\nPatient: It's an absolute fact to me. I know I've said this a few times now, but I'm here because for months I've been seeing this exact same pattern in the project files. It's not an opinion. I can see that certain subcontractors and that one colleague keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. They're doing it so it looks like my quality control failed. And I know IT says the audit trail is clean, but that just means the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. As long as those certain subcontractors and that one colleague keep making those tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps to make my quality control look like it failed, my mind isn't going to change. \n\nLike I mentioned before, even when IT tells me the audit trail is completely clean, I know the record has just been arranged to put the blame on me. So, no, unless they actually stop doing it and the files go back to normal, I don't see why I would think any differently about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that you might be mistaken about the changes to the project files?\n\nPatient: I... no. I know the digital record has been arranged specifically to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever doubt that these changes are being made deliberately to blame you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I mean, like I just mentioned, I work in these project files every single day. I can see the exact pattern where the translation memory and the timestamps are being altered by this colleague and the subcontractors. It's too precise to be an accident or a glitch. \n\nSo when IT comes back and says the audit trail is clean, it doesn't make me doubt what I'm seeing—it just confirms to me that the digital record was carefully arranged to cover their tracks. The whole point is to make my quality control look like it's failing so they can put the blame on me. So... no, I really don't have any doubts about what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't believe your colleague and subcontractors were making these deliberate changes?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not since it started. I mean, before that, everything was fine. But this has been going on for about 11 months now. It all began shortly after I was put in charge of the new localization project. Since that point, it's just been a constant issue, so there hasn't been a time where I didn't believe it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people in other areas of your life are out to get you or harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's strictly related to my work. I don't have any issues like this with my family or friends, or... or anyone else in my personal life. Everything outside of the office is completely fine. It's really just that specific situation with the localization project and those particular people at my job.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about what's happening with the project files ever changed since this started?\n\nPatient: No, they really haven't. I know I'm kind of repeating myself, but for months I’ve just been seeing the exact same pattern in the project files. It hasn't stopped or changed at all. \n\nCertain subcontractors and that one colleague just keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. It's all set up so it looks like my quality control failed. And... well, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn't change what I know. I know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. So, no, my perspective on it has stayed exactly the same since this all began.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could potentially convince you that your colleague and subcontractors are not making these deliberate changes?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think so. I mean, like I just mentioned, the proof is right there in front of me. I've been looking at this exact same pattern for months. I see the tiny changes they make to the translation memory and the timestamps with my own eyes. \n\nSo... if someone tries to tell me it's not happening, or if IT comes back again and says the audit trail is clean, it doesn't really matter. I already know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me and make my quality control look bad. When you can clearly see what they are doing to the files, there's really nothing that can convince you otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other thoughts or beliefs that you think might be hard for others to understand or agree with?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... no. Like I mentioned before, everything else in my life is completely normal. I don't have any unusual ideas or... beliefs about anything else. It's really just this one specific issue at work because I'm dealing with those actual files every day. Outside of that, no, nothing else comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a break or a period of time where you didn't think about these changes to the project files?\n\nPatient: No, there really hasn't been a break. It's been pretty constant. This has been going on for about 11 months now... basically since shortly after I was put in charge of the new localization project. So, no, I haven't really had a period where I wasn't thinking about it, because I have to look at those files every day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the deliberate changes to the project files could ever change?\n\nPatient: I really don't see how it could. I mean... I’m here talking to you because for months I’ve been seeing this exact same pattern in the project files. As long as those certain subcontractors and that one colleague keep making those tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps to make my quality control look like it failed, my mind isn't going to change. \n\nLike I mentioned before, even when IT tells me the audit trail is completely clean, I know the record has just been arranged to put the blame on me. So, no, unless they actually stop doing it and the files go back to normal, I don't see why I would think any differently about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that you might be mistaken about the changes to the project files?\n\nPatient: I... no. I really don't think I'm mistaken. I mean, like I just said, I'm here because for months I've been seeing this exact same pattern in the project files. It's not a mistake. Certain subcontractors and that one colleague are definitely making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. They're doing it specifically so it looks like my quality control failed. And I know IT says the audit trail is clean, but... well, I know the record has just been arranged to put the blame on me. It's too deliberate to be a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the changes to the project files could be considered an opinion or is it an absolute fact to you?\n\nPatient: It's an absolute fact to me. I know I've said this a few times now, but I'm here because for months I've been seeing this exact same pattern in the project files. It's not an opinion. I can see that certain subcontractors and that one colleague keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. They're doing it so it looks like my quality control failed. And I know IT says the audit trail is clean, but that just means the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. So, yes, it's a fact.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people, who are not involved in the project, would see the same pattern in the project files if they looked at them?\n\nPatient: I think... yes, if they knew how our software works, they absolutely would. I know I'm sounding like a broken record today, but I'm only here because for months I’ve been seeing this exact same pattern in the project files. Anyone with the right background would see that certain subcontractors and that one colleague keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. \n\nIt's clearly set up to make my quality control look like it failed. Now, if an outsider just asked our IT department, they might be fooled, because IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean. But like I've been saying, I know the record has just been arranged to put the blame on me. If someone else actually looked at the files themselves and knew what to look for, they'd see exactly what I see.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief about the changes to the project files is something that you think about every day, or are there days when you don't think about it at all?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure if it's every single day. I haven't exactly kept track of how often it crosses my mind. I mostly just think about it when I'm logging in for work and checking the translation memory. On my days off, or when I'm away from my desk, I might not think about it at all. But when I'm working, it's definitely on my mind, just because I have to deal with the files.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief about the changes to the project files affected your relationships with family or friends in any way?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My family and friends aren't involved in work, so it doesn't really come up with them. Outside of this whole project issue, everything else is pretty much normal. I still do my translation work, manage my team's workflow, pay the bills, and keep up with my regular family routines. \n\nThe only time things really get off track or tense is at work, specifically when the file history or those certain coworkers come up. But at home? No, it hasn't changed anything with my family or friends.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment, no matter how brief, when you questioned or doubted your belief about the changes to the project files?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... maybe for a brief second when IT first told me the audit trail was clean, I paused and wondered if I was missing something. But then I just go back and look at the files. \n\nLike I said earlier, I've been seeing this exact same pattern for months now. It's always those certain subcontractors and that one colleague making these tiny little changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. It's just so obvious that it's being done to make my quality control look like it failed. So, even when IT insists the system is clean, I know what I'm seeing. The record has been carefully arranged to put the blame on me. Because the proof is right there in front of me every time I log in, I don't really have any reason to doubt it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like other people, outside of this work situation, are watching you or plotting against you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt anything like that. Outside of work, I don't feel like anyone is watching me or plotting against me. It's really just the situation with the translation memory and those specific people at work. I don't have any concerns about my neighbors or strangers on the street or anything like that. Everything else in my life feels completely normal.\n\nDoctor: Has your conviction about the deliberate changes to the project files ever wavered, even for a short time, since you first noticed them?\n\nPatient: I think we just touched on this a moment ago, but... no, it hasn't. Other than that one brief second with the IT report I mentioned earlier, my certainty hasn't wavered at all. \n\nI work in those files every day. I see the altered translation memory and the timestamps with my own eyes. When you're looking right at the evidence over and over like that, there's just no reason to second-guess it.\n\nDoctor: Has your conviction about the deliberate changes to the project files remained the same since you first started telling others about it?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's remained exactly the same. I know I keep repeating myself, but it's because nothing has changed. Like I said, I'm here because for months I've been seeing this exact same pattern in the project files. \n\nThose certain subcontractors and that one colleague just keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. It's done specifically so it looks like my quality control failed. And, you know, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I know what I'm looking at.
